# Feedback notes

Running list of feedback on Adequate Audits. Newest round at the top; items are checked off when done.

## Next up (your order)
- [x] **Open a log or live log without saving anything (your question, Oct 19; done in round 259).** One small, quiet link
  under Start now and under Welcome Back!, "Just open a log ›", opens the Open a raid night window (paste a report or live
  log link, a combat log on your PC, or a real raid night). Checked: opening a log never adds a team or character. Only the
  setup, "Add a team or character", "You raid with…" and "Which one is you?" do. Opening your own raid's report only keeps
  its pull summaries in this browser, so your team's page counts that night.
- [x] **A high-expectations raider's pass (your note, Oct 18; fixed in round 243): what would lose a serious Mythic player.** Walked a real
  night (Adequate, Ula'tek Mythic, 6 wipes at 89–96% in P1, plus The Coiled Altar kill) on every tab, one pull and the
  whole night, desktop and phone.
  - **Trust breakers:** grades too kind and in two versions (the raid A and Mechanics S 98 on Players, but Mechanics B 81
    on Recap, for the same night; "Report card" means two different things on those pages); grades that contradict their
    own line ("Avoidable damage A: went wrong in 4 of 5 pulls", "Caustic Waves F" for mostly could-be-better); Power
    Infusion advice that contradicts itself ("Simonebiles was the better pick" next to a ranking with Dwarfdude above
    Simonebiles, and "next pull give it to Dwarfdude"); "Ula&#39;tek" shown raw on the night's Best pull tile.
  - **Noise a raider tunes out:** "Spend your mana" for healers and "Drink your combat potion" on a 1:42 P1 wipe; resource
    capping as the first thing to "Talk to" four DPS about on a wipe caused by mechanics; healing per second against top
    parses on a short wipe; "Always be casting 77%: Good" for Mythic; the 0:00–0:05 pre-pull gap counted as idle; an
    Interrupts tile saying "Nothing to kick"; two tiles that both say "No NSRT note".
  - **Readability:** lines crammed with brackets ("Egg 1: broke into a Viper (Noxious Splash with Flahozinho) (picked up
    at 1:35, held for 5 s) (and 1 more)"), full numbers like 5,090,214 instead of 5.1M, "went over the limit" without
    saying what the limit is, "3 times: got hit 15 times".
  - **First look:** a report opens on the night's last boss (Ula'tek, pull 6), not the boss you're progressing or killed.
  - **Phone:** on Mechanics the problems start at the bottom of the first screen (Show one player and What went well
    come first), and the maps in What went wrong are 63 pixels wide.
  - **Already great:** opens in about a second; deaths explained with the defensive that was still ready; the wipe filter
    is honest about what it left out; Rotation's "worth about 14.79M healing" fixes; "wipes lasted longer, 1:07 → 1:36".
- [ ] **Assignments: when the roster doesn't match the note, offer to work out the stand-ins' assignments from the pull and
  fix the note (your note, Oct 18).** My take: solid, as an upgrade of what's there: This pull already offers "Make the
  new note" and guesses each stand-in from the same cooldown pressed, kicks, or the same role. The upgrade reads the whole
  pull to see what each stand-in really did (who soaked which group, who kicked in that slot, who pressed the cooldown at
  that time) and fills in every job from that, saying how sure it is, before you copy the new note.
- [ ] **Review where the links to other parts of the site sit, for one cohesive experience (your note, Oct 18: "things
  feel a bit spread out, maybe even redundant").** My take: amazing, and worth doing before more raiders arrive. A first
  look at the top-right menu and the Your teams button: "Start screen" does what the Debrief logo and Home do; "Paste a
  link" and "Open combat log" repeat the front page's boxes; "Saved progress" overlaps Your teams; "Tips for this page" and
  "Take the tour" could be one Help item. Plan: list every link and button that moves you somewhere (top bar, menu, front
  page, pull banner, team pages, "See it in" buttons), group them by where they go, mock up one tidier layout (each place
  reachable one clear way, the menu short) for your OK, then build it.
  **Review written (Oct 18): notes/Getting around review.html**, 16 rough spots with mockups. The big ones: Back always
  leaves Debrief; the boss is picked in three places; "All pulls" means three things; on a phone, navigation takes 57% of
  the first screen. Option A (keep the tabs, drop the repeats) or Option B (my pick: pick a pull or the whole night, then
  a tab). Waiting for your pick.
- [x] **Adding a team or character fills in the rest (your note, Oct 18; done in round 239).** Add a team, and it asks which
  player is you (and adds your character page). Add a character, and it asks right away about the teams that logged
  your raid nights.
- [ ] **Discord: example posts and how-to's in the channels (your note, Oct 18).** My take: good; empty channels put people
  off posting, and one example shows what a good post looks like. Suggestions: a pinned "How to report a bug" in
  #bug-reports with a short fill-in layout (boss, pull link, what you saw, what you expected); one real example post each
  in #questions and #suggestions (with the right tag); a one-line description at the top of #general-chat. Shown to you
  before anything is posted.
- [x] **Spruce up the Discord before launch (your note, Oct 18; done: patch notes, FAQ, What is Debrief, welcome fixed).** My take: right priority, it's the first place new
  raiders land. Suggestions: a short "What is Debrief" post with three pictures (a map, What went wrong, the Assignments
  how-to); the first patch notes in #updates covering today's work (drafted for your OK first); a small pinned FAQ in the
  questions channel; tidier channel descriptions and emojis. Every post is shown to you before it goes up.
- [ ] **Maps first: raiders really love the mini maps (your note, Oct 18, now high priority).** My take: agreed, they're
  what people remember about Debrief, so show them off. Three parts:
  1. Every mini map on Mechanics shows open, with no drop-down (done in round 209, with the tour leading with maps).
  2. Make them stand out on the mechanics where a map tells the story (soaks, spreads, baits, adds): a small map right in
     "What went wrong" for that mechanic (done in round 210), and a map for the first death on Pull recap (done in round 218). The
     "Map" mark on rows was dropped: the maps show on their own now.
  3. **Cast bars on the maps (your note, Oct 18; done in round 217):** an add, boss or other enemy casting something that should be kicked
     (or that the note assigns a kick for) gets a cast bar under its nameplate, like in game. It fills as the cast goes,
     turns green with the kicker's name when it's kicked, and red when it goes off. My take: amazing; it makes the kick
     maps read like the game. The log has when each cast started and when it was kicked or finished, so the bar can fill
     in time on the animated maps, and show where the cast stood at that moment on the still ones.
  5. **Drawings that look like the real mechanic (your note, Oct 18):** when we can, each mechanic is drawn the way it looks
     in game, in its colour (a green venom puddle, a purple shadow circle) with its spell's icon at the centre (loaded at
     play time like every icon, never bundled). My take: good; people recognise a mechanic by its look faster than by a label.
  4. ~~Sharper maps: our own clean outline of each boss room.~~ **Declined (your call, Oct 19): keep the boss platform
     picture as it is.** A mockup (notes/Room outline mockup - The Coiled Altar.jpg) showed the plain outline loses the
     look of the room.
- [x] **A short how-to on the Assignments tab (your note, Oct 18; done in round 211):** it's basically empty until a note is pasted, and no
  other website does this, so it needs explaining clearly to stand out. My take: strong, an empty page loses people. My
  suggestion: when no note is pasted yet, the page shows three plain steps (copy your raid's NSRT note, paste it here,
  Debrief checks every assignment on every pull: soaks, kicks, orbs, cooldowns), a small picture of what the results look
  like, and a "Try it with an example" button that fills in a sample note so people see it work before they have their
  own. The Assignments tips and the tour step point at it too.

- [x] **Top players: fastest kills plus good parses (built in round 189, with your output-from-parses note):** the bars and "Top raids" marks now
  come from every player of the spec in the fastest Mythic kills (speed, not parses), so they show how players on the best
  teams play the real fight. My suggestion: keep the fastest kills but count only the players in them who also parsed well
  (75 or higher), for real fights and strong individual play at once. The catch: rare specs have fewer players left, so their
  bars rest on less data. Holy Paladin's marks still come from an earlier set of 10 kills: move it to the same set.
- [x] **Spec checks on every boss (my suggestion; done in round 189):** every bar and "Top raids" mark comes from Twin Fangs. The other bosses

- [x] **Saved NSRT notes (your note, Oct 18):** done in round 195. Save several notes, and pick the saved one that matches the pull's exact
  raid composition (or have Debrief pick it), or edit an existing note in place for small composition changes (swapping a
  name). My take: very useful for raid leaders, and it fits how notes change week to week. Matching by roster is the easy
  part; editing in place should stay small (swap a player for another), so the note doesn't drift from the one in game.
- [x] **Minimaps must show the mechanic itself (your note, Oct 18):** done in round 195. Every minimap that highlights a mechanic has to draw
  where the mechanic is, not only where the players stood. If its location can't be drawn properly and accurately, remove
  that map. My take: agreed, a map with only player dots looks like proof but doesn't show anything. A log can place players,
  bosses and adds (anything that is a unit in the log), and mechanics centred on a player (a spread circle around whoever had
  the debuff). It can't place ground effects that aren't units, like most puddles. So: go through every map, keep the ones
  that can draw their mechanic, and remove the rest.

- [ ] **Compare raid vs raid: mechanical execution (your note, Oct 19).** Your raid against another raid on the same boss,
  mechanic by mechanic: how many soaks were missed, who got hit by what, deaths and what caused them, for each raid.
  My take: **amazing**, and the best use for the parked "Similar raid groups". Debrief can already open any report and run
  its full Mechanics check, so the other raid's kill can be read the same way as yours and put side by side (their
  mistakes per mechanic against yours). It needs their whole kill downloaded and checked, so it's slower than the
  current Compare (about as long as opening a pull). Pairs well with picking a raid with a group like yours.
- [ ] **A Comparison tab (your note, Oct 19; this gathers the Compare notes below and the "comparison tab" note under the header):**
  its own tab with three ways to compare: (1) you against a teammate, (2) your raid against other raids with a similar
  group (the same classes and specs, or close), found by browsing or searching, and (3) you against the top players of
  your spec on the same boss. My take: **amazing** overall, and the order matters. Top players of your spec is the most
  useful and the cheapest, since the app already pulls top players for the Rotation bars. A teammate is easy, the data is
  already in your log. Browsing similar groups is **solid but the hardest**: Warcraft Logs can't search by group makeup,
  so we'd pull a batch of top reports per boss and sort them by how close their classes and specs are to yours. Build it
  in that order, one at a time.
- [ ] **Performance: a Compare option** (your note; now part of the Comparison tab above): put two things side by side somewhere in the Performance tab (for example
  two players, a player against their own best pull, or this pull against an earlier one). Where it goes: part of the
  Performance talk.
  **Update (your note, Oct 17):** a comparison area: your logs against other logs (a top guild's on the same boss), and
  yourself against other people on your team. My take: start with you against a teammate of the same spec and against
  your own best pull, then your raid against one of the top guilds above on the same boss.
  **Update (your note, Oct 18):** a Compare button against top guilds with a similar raid composition (the same classes and

- [x] The desktop app (.exe) with auto-update (plan in notes/Desktop app plan.md). **Removed for now (your call, Oct 18).**
  **Update (your note, Oct 18):** the desktop app is only for live logging: it reads your WoWCombatLog.txt during the raid
  and sends it to the website. Answer: the analysis stays the same. The website already reads raw combat logs (the same
  format the app reads from a file), and Warcraft Logs reports are turned into that same format first. What's new is the
  sending: the app itself, a place on the website to receive and keep the log (a night's raw log is hundreds of MB, so it
  goes in compressed pieces), and a way for the raid to open the live night. Parses still need the log on Warcraft Logs.
  **My take (Oct 18): probably not worth building.** Warcraft Logs' own uploader already live-logs during raid, and Debrief's
  "Live" switch already checks that report every 30 seconds for new pulls. A Debrief app would rebuild what raiders already
  have, plus storage costs. It's only worth it if live Warcraft Logs isn't fast enough, or some raiders won't use Warcraft Logs.

## 2026-10-18, round 215: Easier on tanks in bad stuff; Heroic Anguished Cry checked
- [x] **Tanks standing in bad stuff is only a note now (your note).** A tank hit by avoidable damage (a pool, a wave, a
  circle) is a small note, "Tanks often take this on purpose, so it's only a note.", not a mistake. It's still a mistake
  when it killed them, when they kept standing in it (6 or more hits, or twice what counts as bad for everyone else), or
  when it's a tank mechanic (those rules judge tanks on purpose). In the Avoidable damage section their row says "tank
  (only a note)" or "tank, kept standing in it". Checked on the test night: Living Venom, Plague Wave, Umbral Ejection,
  Evil Eyes, Aftershock, Tempest and Caustic Claws on tanks became notes; Domideus taking Tempest 7 times stayed a mistake.
- [x] **Heroic Ula'tek's Anguished Cry, checked (your screenshot).** Heroic runs it like Mythic: the Weakened Doomscale
  casts it, it can be kicked, and good groups kick every one (your Heroic kill night: over 20 kicks, none got through).
  So the Mythic rule judges Heroic pulls fairly. "Got off" only counts a cast that really finished; one stopped by a stun
  or by the add dying isn't counted.
- [x] **The yards were right.** On the Heroic kill night every kicker's spot and the add's were logged within half a
  second of the cast (melee kickers stood 1-10 yards away, ranged 18-22). So "Icefrosting (31 yd)" meant Icefrosting
  really stood 31 yards from the Doomscale. The pull in your screenshot isn't in the reports I have, so I couldn't open
  that exact row.
- [x] **The kick rotation now says when someone was too far for their kick:** "kick rotation: Xyron (45 yd), Bentwo
  (53 yd, too far for Disrupt)". It uses the kick each player had been using on that add (Kick, Pummel and Rebuke reach
  5 yards, Disrupt 10, Mind Freeze 15, Quell 25, Wind Shear and Silence 30, Counterspell and Counter Shot 40), with 8 yards
  of slack for the add's size. A Warlock's kick comes from their pet, so it's never called too far. Yards only show when
  both spots were logged in the last 3 seconds, so an old spot is never measured.

## 2026-10-18, round 194: Devourer's checks, and an honest line on thin spec pages (your note)
- [x] **No more "Nice work" over a low parse:** when every check on a spec page is good but the parse is under 50 (or, with
  no parse, the output is well under the top parses), the page now says "Your checks look good, but your parse is 35, so
  the gap is in things these checks don't cover yet," instead of praising it. This covers every spec with few checks.
- [x] **Devourer, researched in full** (the Wowhead and Icy Veins guides and the SimulationCraft list), then checked against
  the top Devourers' kills:
  - **Void Metamorphosis windows were read wrong:** the log has two buffs both called Void Metamorphosis (the soul counter
    and the form), so the page counted 11 "windows" where there were 5. The windows now come from the Collapsing Star
    counter, which is only up in the form. 3 or more Collapsing Stars counts as done right (the top players get 4 in most
    full windows; the guides aim for 4 to 5), with fewer needed in a window the pull cut short.
  - **New: Void Ray in Void Metamorphosis, a minute.** Inside the form, Void Ray is free and pauses the Fury drain. The bar
    follows each boss's top players, since bosses with more movement leave less room (about 7 a minute on Twin Fangs and
    Vashnik, about 4 on Kith'ix): 92% of theirs is good, 82% could be better, and a boss without enough top logs is shown
    but not graded. This is what separated Dashtoswirly (parse 35, 4.9 a minute against the top players' 7.0) from them.
  - Reap and Cull at 4 or more souls also follows each boss (the top players sit at 69% on the Coiled Altar, with its adds).
  - "Top players" marks for Devourer were measured again with the new checks, from the same players (parse 75 or higher,
    fastest kills) on each boss.
  - **New: Reap and Cull at 4 or more souls**, **Soulburst spent** (the tier set's proc), and **Fury wasted outside the
    form** (inside it, Fury is a timer, so it doesn't count). The old "Void Ray on cooldown" check was removed: outside the
    form Void Ray has no cooldown, it costs 100 Fury.
  - **How long Void Metamorphosis sat ready** before you went in is shown on each window, but not graded: the top players
    also hold it for 6 to 35 seconds a kill (Bloodlust, the boss's phases).
  - The top Devourers now score 88 to 100, and Dashtoswirly 84 instead of 100.

## 2026-10-17, round 140: Twin Fangs checks, fairer grades, a polish pass
- [x] **Twin Fangs Feast check said clean on Heroic** (my #2): Heroic has no Tainted Blood founts at all (Warcraft Logs lists
  no Tainted Blood or Tainted Burst anywhere in a Heroic night), so it graded something that never happened. It only shows
  when a pull had founts now (Mythic); on Mythic it was right all along (19 or 20 players absorbing each Feast).
- [x] **Twin Fangs soaks, checked against the guides** (your question): two different soaks.
  - Ravenous Feast: three strikes, one per group (right, left, melee), 4+ each, Feasted after one. Already checked right.
  - Caustic Globules (Mythic): Method says 10 a set, 10 players each, the other 10 the next set. Your pulls have 15 a set
    for 20 players, so about half the raid has to soak two sets in a row. That stays shown; it's now flagged when it's
    taken at high venom (the stack budget or above), since the raid leader keeps high-stack players out.
  - Lethal stacks: the guides disagree (Method 10, Mythic Trap 9 on Mythic). Your pulls had players living at 9: it stays 10.
  - Barbed Bulwark: "broken after 6 s" stays. Mythic Trap says globules explode after 10 s, so that leaves under 4 s to soak.
- [x] **Fairer mechanics grades** (my #4), after adding up every mistake on the Coiled Altar night:
  - An assigned external or Zephyr counts double in the share of assignments done; a healing cooldown reminder counts half
    (they're timed around raid damage that doesn't land on the exact second, and healers have 5 or 6 a pull).
  - Mutagenic Venom: brushing past a purple carrier once is minor (no points); hit twice or more, it's medium.
  - Looked at and kept: Axegrinder (5+ separate walk-ins is medium), Sever on a non-tank (serious), dying in a Guillotine soak.
  - Your grade on the night: 82 → 88; Huservoker, who missed their assigned Guillotine external most pulls, went down.
- [x] **Polish pass** (your #5): orb pickups say "sets", not "waves"; "both" instead of "all 2"; US spelling; the wipe
  filter line reads the same on every tab. Phones: Assignments' boxes were squeezed into 6 columns (now 2), "What went
  wrong" lines wrap instead of being cut off, a row's description goes under its title, and a ✓ or ✕ no longer sits on
  its own line. Desktop: headers like "Overheal" and the Summary's grade boxes are no longer cut off.

  SimulationCraft's raid report (MID2, updated Oct 17) has every DPS and tank spec's actions per minute on a target dummy,
  by hero talents (Fury Warrior 72, Havoc 66 or 87, Marksmanship 40…). On your pulls good players sit at 90-105% of it, so

  Evokers aren't simmed: they're measured against their own other pulls and the same spec.

## 2026-10-17, round 128: wasted resources, casts that didn't finish, gaps standing still or moving
- [x] **Wasted resources** (your pick): each player's power other than mana (Energy, Rage, Focus, Runic Power, runes, Holy

  full while it kept building is wasted ("Spend your Focus before it caps: at full for 30 s, 9 times, the longest from
  1:34"). 3% of the time alive or less is full marks. A number read as they pressed a spender is from just before paying
  it, so spending at full never counts. (Warcraft Logs only records combo points on finishers, so those can't be judged
  from a report; a combat log from your PC has them.)

  of 35 (14%), about 8 s lost: Frostbolt ×4 · 5 of them while moving"). Not counted: one cut off by a death, mind control
  or the end of the pull, an instant proc that logs its start and cast together, or one replaced within 0.4 s. Healers
  count it lightly (cancelling a heal that would only overheal is good play).
- [x] **Casting gaps, standing still or moving** (your pick): each gap of 4 s or more is marked from the player's
  positions, and the fix fits: "Keep casting while standing still" (nothing was in the way) or "Keep casting on the
  move" (instants and movement abilities).
- [x] Raid-wide downtime, wider: when at least half of the DPS and tanks are stopped for 3 s or more (everyone running
  for a mechanic, a transition), that time isn't held against anyone.

## 2026-10-17, round 125: Performance, the trust pass (grades fair before the raid sees them)
- [x] **Every cooldown checked**: all 203 listed cooldowns (big cooldowns, defensives, externals) against Wowhead's
  tooltips for the live patch, then against the real presses in Wolfthorn's pulls. 34 were off, mostly Midnight changes:
  Summon Demonic Tyrant 1 min (not 1.5), Summon Infernal 2 min, Pillar of Frost 45 s, Army of the Dead 1.5 min, Fire and
  Storm Elemental 2 min, Doom Winds 1 min, Metamorphosis 2 min, Shadow Blades 1.5 min, Icebound Fortitude 2 min, Ardent
  Defender 1.5 min, Guardian of Ancient Kings 3 min, Gift of the Naaru 2 min; and longer: Lay on Hands 10 min, Holy Word:
  Salvation 12 min, Killing Spree 3 min, Fortifying Brew 6 min (talents shorten these). Your raid's presses matched the new
  numbers (Tyrant pressed every 61 s, Pillar every 45 s, Army every 91 s). Empower Rune Weapon is out: two charges on a
  30 s recharge now, not a big cooldown.
- [x] Base cooldowns are the rule: talents only shorten cooldowns, so "possible uses" never asks for more than a player
  really had, and something only counts as "still ready" at a death once even its base cooldown was back.
- [x] **Forced downtime left out**: stretches of 4 s or more when three in four of the DPS and tanks cast nothing (the
  boss gone, a transition) don't count against anyone's casting, and their gaps there aren't listed. Healers are judged as
  usual. Example: the first 10 s of Ula'tek's intermission.
- [x] **Raid cooldowns against the plan**: a cooldown your NSRT note assigns (Aura Mastery, Avenging Wrath, Revival,
  Rewind, Darkness…) is judged by the note: on time is full marks, a few seconds off 70%, more than 8 s off 40%, missed
  none. A raid cooldown the note doesn't assign is fine at half its possible uses (it waits for the big damage).
  Cooldowns now weigh by the fight time they cover, so a missed 3-minute raid cooldown counts for more than one press of a
  1-minute one.

## 2026-10-17, round 116: boss review (Twin Fangs, Coiled Altar, Ula'tek), the website in Chrome
Checked on your team's logs (Twin Fangs Mythic Oct 14, 30 pulls; Coiled Altar Mythic Oct 15, 32 pulls; Heroic Sep 2: Twin Fangs kill,
Coiled Altar wipe and kill, 11 Ula'tek pulls) and, for game facts only, a top Coiled Altar Mythic kill and the Ula'tek test logs. No
errors anywhere, every phase found. Fixed:
- [x] **The website in Chrome** (your note: "Failed to fetch" for team and Warcraft Logs pulls, fine in Edge): ad blockers block web
  addresses ending in "/api/events", which is where the site downloaded each pull. It now downloads from "/api/fightlog". Retries
  also skip the browser's saved copy, and the error says what went wrong (and to allow the site in an ad blocker).
- [x] **Memory**: every Warcraft Logs pull you opened stayed in memory for the whole visit (100+ MB for a long Mythic pull), so
  browsing a few dozen pulls crashed the tab. A pull is let go once it's saved on the PC.
- [x] **Twin Fangs, Coiling Ichor**: ~18 small mistakes a pull (634 on 30 pulls; 50 on your kill). A hit is 50 to 75K and adds no
  Eternal Venom (checked on your kill), and two circles overlapping counted each tick twice. Now a tick counts once, and only a
  stand-in of 5+ ticks is a (minor) mistake: 69 on 30 pulls.
- [x] **Twin Fangs, globule soaks**: an extra globule at low venom isn't flagged (sets had more globules than soakers: 15 for 13 on
  your kill). One taken at the stack budget or above still is.
- [x] **Twin Fangs, Twin balance**: shown, not judged. The Mythic plan in the guides kills one Fang first on purpose, and your kill
  had them die 21s apart with 8 Uncoiled Wrath stacks, so it read red on a kill.
- [x] **Twin Fangs, Avoidable damage**: drawn as bars like every other boss (Stir the Depths water, Sanguine Storm).
- [x] **Coiled Altar on Heroic**: the orbs were judged against the Mythic plan (5 to 6 per frontal, purples) though Heroic has no
  purples; the Heroic plan is to clear every orb, so the count is shown, not judged. A Fragment reaching Zul'jan heals 3% on Heroic
  (10% on Mythic): amber, not red, and a row says so when nobody blocked.
- Covered already, checked: Twin Fangs' Broodling kicks (Visceral Burst, by side); Ula'tek's egg failures (Putrid Membrane is the
  hatch's raid damage); Toxic Fumes and Necrotic Vapors are constant raid damage (nothing to judge).

- [x] Your guild's only Mythic Ula'tek pulls are the 6 from Oct 15 (all early Stage 1), so the check used the fastest Mythic kill on Warcraft Logs (another guild, xw649bM3KCvFtDqn: a 70% wipe and the kill), for game facts only. No errors; the stages land at 2:54, 5:19 and 6:12; the data check found 29 of the 34 tracked spells, and the 5 missing are ones nobody triggered (or the lower-difficulty egg shell). Your 6 pulls re-run clean too.
- [x] **Bug fixed**: a Wretch's first Fester Burst has a 4-second cast bar, and its start and landing sometimes counted as two bubbles, so two Wretches read "3 bubbles" when they had 2 (confirmed against the raw casts). Starts and landings within 9 seconds now count once; the Warden's Writhing Gestation count got the same fix (its 3-second cast bar never tripped it, but it could).
- [x] Checked against the top 10 Mythic kills, the other flags hold: Unchecked Rage at 7:33 (20 hit) is a real mistake (none of the other 9 took it); egg carriers with 5 Blight Vein stacks in Stage 2 are avoidable (7 of 10 kills had none); Shriekers reaching Boiling Venom (killed at 28–29 s, it boils at 25 s); a Caustic Waves death confirmed in the death log.
- [x] Revenge, researched: the surviving Weakened Doomscale gets +100% haste and damage, so its Anguished Cry (542K to everyone within 60 yards and a 6 s stun, interruptible) becomes about 1.1M on a 1.25 s cast, and its Desperate Thrash frontal doubles too. In the top 10 Mythic kills, Revenge lasted from under a second to 17 s, and not one Anguished Cry got through while it was up (every one kicked); the cost was extra frontal hits. So it's now rated on what it cost, not how long it lasted: any Revenge is "!", and it's "✕" when an Anguished Cry got through or someone died to that Doomscale while it had Revenge. (That second path didn't happen in any top kill, so it's untested on real data.) The module now tracks Anguished Cry hits.

## 2026-10-16, round 75: Performance, six new ways to read a pull
- [x] A view switch under the timeline: **Players** (the bars and the picked player, as before), **Phases**, **Damage windows**, **Targets**, **Healers** (Healing only) and **Heatmap**. A name in any view opens that player on Players.
- [x] **Phases**: a row per player, a column per phase plus the whole pull, per second while alive, each cell shaded by rank in its column. Once other pulls are read, ▲▼ shows each cell against that player's best pull of the night in that phase (★ when this is it; only for phases at least two pulls reached).
- [x] **Damage windows**: Bloodlust's 40 seconds, plus the fight's own: Rage of the Shackled (Ula'tek), each Eternal Nightfall shield until it broke, and the Soulbinding intermission (Coiled Altar). Per player: damage per second in it (damage soaked by a boss shield counts, checked against the fight audit: 35M into shield 1 either way), ×N against their own rate, the big cooldown and combat potion they had going. Starts on DPS.
- [x] **Targets**: raid damage by target over time (stacked), each player's split between the boss (and what shares its health), the adds that have to die, and everything else, then each set of those adds: how long it lived and how fast the DPS got on it after the raid's first hit. Lists: Spiteful Soulcoiler (Coiled Altar); Doomscale Warden, Weakened Doomscale, Shrieker, Wretch and Rawling (Ula'tek); other bosses fall back to any add with 3% of the damage. Long name lists collapse to a count (Stage 2's split sides made them noise).
- [x] **Heatmap**: a strip per player, a cell per 5 seconds, against their own rate (Damage taken: one scale for all), with dead time, big cooldowns (defensives on Damage taken), deaths, phases and Bloodlust marked.
- [x] **Healers**: healing per second, overhealing (shown, not counted), the share of heals that landed on someone under half health, and from a real combat log the healers' mana over the pull (lowest point, mana left at the end or death, mana potions). Warcraft Logs reports don't carry mana; the panel says so.
- [x] **The picked player**: their casts under the timeline on the same time axis (key buffs as bars, big cooldowns, most-cast spells), an ability table (casts, hits, crit, average, share), buffs by uptime with who gave them (raid buffs folded away), mana for healers, and "Compared": their best pull so far, others of their spec this pull, and on a Warcraft Logs kill their parse.
- [x] **Parses**: on a Warcraft Logs kill, each bar carries the player's parse in Warcraft Logs' colours (wipes have none). Works through the local helper; the hosted Worker got an `/api/rankings` route (needs a redeploy to go live).
- [x] The Ula'tek Wretch: researched. The guides only cover the beam and the bubble, but the 10 fastest Mythic kills on Warcraft Logs all handle it the same way: each Wretch gets exactly two Fester Bursts and dies 72–95 seconds after it lands. The bubbles run on a fixed clock (about 36, 68 and 100 seconds after landing), so it dies on a timer, between the second and the third bubble, not on sight. It's off the switch-speed list now (its damage still counts as useful), and the Mechanics "How it works" gives the bubble timings. The existing check (kill it before the third bubble) already matches.

## 2026-10-15, round 44: maps on real logs, Gloombomb check (raid night)
- [x] Maps showed the floor but no players, orbs or bosses on the user's own combat log: the real log writes positionX (north) then positionY (west), and the Warcraft Logs translator had written WCL x/y in that order instead, so the maps were calibrated on swapped axes. Checked: the user's pull 7 and the KGy report draw the same, and both first frontals face the entrance.

## 2026-10-15, round 42: live logging without a picker
- [x] Root cause: WoW lives under C:\Program Files (x86), and Chrome won't let a page pick files there (system folder), so neither the file nor the folder picker worked.
- [x] The local helper (server.ps1) now finds the WoW Logs folder itself (registry, then common install paths; a logs-path.txt next to start.bat overrides it) and serves the newest WoWCombatLog read-only (/log/info, /log/read; only WoWCombatLog*.txt names, opened so WoW keeps writing). "Follow my combat log" on the start screen uses it, shows which log it found, and switches when WoW starts a new file. The file and folder pickers stay as fallbacks (and for the hosted site).
- Tested on the real raid log in progress: pulls 1 (7:24 PM) and 2 (7:29 PM) appeared and opened. Needs start.bat restarted once to load the new helper.
- [x] The first restart didn't take: the 11:17 AM helper was still running and held the port, so the new one couldn't start. Restarted it; live logging confirmed in the user's tab (pulls 1 to 3). start.bat now closes any old helper before starting.

## 2026-10-15, round 41: live logging from the combat log file
- [x] Live logging now picks the WoWCombatLog file itself ("Choose your combat log"); the Logs folder is still an option underneath (follows new log files after a relog).
- [x] Fixed a likely cause of live logging failing in raid: WoW writes to the log nonstop, and Chrome refuses to read a file snapshot once the file has changed (NotReadableError). Reads now take a fresh snapshot per 8 MB chunk with retries. Pulls are also read the same way when opened.
- [x] Read errors now show in the sidebar and on the waiting screen (before, "checked …" overwrote them, so it failed silently).
- Tested with a simulated growing 55 MB log of two real pulls, 30% of reads failing: both pulls appeared with correct durations and the recap opened automatically.

## 2026-10-15, round 15: Coiled Altar Mythic audit against the Tactyks guide

Read the full guide (Mythic Coiled Altar Guide and Fight Walkthrough, YouTube, Sep 2026) and checked every rule against all 7 Coiled Altar pulls, including the kill. App text stays neutral (no guide names).
- [x] Guillotine: needs at least 5 on Mythic (3 on Heroic/Normal); was 6 / 5. A soak with 4 wiped or killed people on pulls 1 and 3, which matches. Soak plan text rewritten (G1 = 2nd soak's immuners + tanky players, externals on target; G2 = tanks + 2 immunities + BoP on target; Grim 1 own spot, Grim 2 and 3 together; Grim 3 = over-send, no protection needed). New: immuners of the 2nd soak should also soak the 1st. Zephyr counts as protection. Unprotected target = amber, red if they died. Fixed: the Died column was red on every row.
- [x] Orbs per frontal: 5 to 6, never more than 7 (was "3 purple + 3-4 green, then 2 purple"). P1: second-to-last frontal 2 to 3, last one none. P3: first frontal takes every purple.
- [x] Fixed false alarms: the orb explosion when Zul'jan dies was counted as the last frontal popping 10 orbs plus a "purple collision"; a P1 Sever cut off by the push was counted. P3 mass stacks right after Defilement of the Coiled Altar now say "Defilement detonated the orbs still on the ground" (pull 3's wipe), not a collision.
- [x] P1 push: dropped the "push as Fangs starts" check (every pull, including the kill, pushed ~24s before Fangs). Now: stacks after the push (plan 9 to 11) and raid cooldowns stacked.
- [x] Intermission: 3 to 5 Spirit Erasure stacks per round, up to 10 when a raid cooldown is up (was "stop at 4"). New row: Zul'jan health when P3 starts (under 35% = on pace).
- [x] P3 ending: kill Zul'jan first with Malacrass at ~7 to 8%, then Malacrass (was "die within 5s of each other"). Kill: Zul'jan died with Malacrass at 4%.
- [x] Adds: first kick judged against the Gloombomb cast (kick once it starts), not "early on odd sets"; alive when the next set spawns = amber (the kill did this every set), alive two sets later = red; the last set of a phase isn't judged.
- [x] New: Eternal Nightfall shields (the P2/P3 damage check): broken in Xs, red if the cast finishes. Kill: all 4 broken in 14 to 16s.
- [x] Dreadmarch: stack on the raid's marker; new check for a ghost spawning far from the clump (someone moved before it spawned). Missed ghosts in Soul Sever = amber (walk it back for the next one).
- Not in the guide, unchanged: Venomfang dispels (still flags anything over 4s; open question from round 2).
- [x] Found and fixed: long pulls weren't saved on this PC (a 10-minute Mythic pull is ~514,000 events, ~140 MB; the browser refuses one saved value over ~133 MB, silently). Now saved in parts of 100,000 events; the kill reopens from disk instead of re-downloading.
- Note: the test report is a random public log, not our raid. Strategy rules come from the guide; the log only checks game facts (IDs, sizes, timings).

## 2026-10-15, round 14

- [x] Each death says why they died and what would have prevented it (Deaths rows: "What would have helped"; "Why + recap" opens the full explanation above the hits). Built from the last 10 seconds: killing hit and how fast health fell, biggest damage sources, little healing, the player's own mechanic mistakes just before (with their fix), avoidable boss spells, uninterrupted casts that hit them, room damage (e.g. Toxic Fumes), and defensives / Healthstone / potion still ready.
- Fixed on the way:
  - Death recaps only kept the last 16 events (under a second in heavy raid damage), so "X% to 0 in Ns" was wrong. Now the full last 12 seconds, measured from the last moment they were at 90%+.
  - Interrupts missed finished casts: a Visceral Burst that got off was counted as kicked because the add's next cast was kicked. Cast-bar spells that finish now always count as got off (pull 2: 1 at 1:45, which caused most of the deaths; kill: 1).
- [ ] Question: with the wipe filter at 2, Coiled Altar pull 3 (8:16 wipe at 16.9%) is cut at 0:40, hiding 167 results, because two players died early and the raid kept going.

## 2026-10-15, round 11

- [x] Corrosive Spit logic audited against the raw log of all 5 Twin Fangs pulls; the app now matches exactly (aims 28/14/32/11/33, spits fired 19/9/22/6/20, bystanders 0/4/2/2/0).
  - Findings: every aim ends in a finished cast or the add's death; no spit ever switched targets; every hit traces back to the add that cast it.
  - The target isn't always hit: standing right on the add (~1–6 yd) the line can miss them and still hit people further down it (pull 2 0:44, pull 4 1:43).
  - Fixed: aim ends are matched by add and player (two adds can aim at one player); removed a rule that could skip a bystander who had their own aim from another add.
- [x] Table is one row per spit ("Spawn 1, spit 2"), with its own map and a result: fired and hit the target / fired and missed the target / add died first, no spit. An add's 2nd or 3rd spit is flagged ("the add should have been dead"). Pull 5 set 4 = Spawn 1 fired twice at two players (4:18 and 4:25), which is why it showed two targets.

A spit now counts as fired only when the add finishes the cast or the target takes the hit (the old "aim lasted ~4.5s" guess was wrong). Same fix corrects pulls 2–4.
- [x] Columns renamed: Spits fired / Aimed at (not at fault) / Hit without being targeted / Add died.

The target isn't at fault (the line moves with them), but anyone else hit by the line made a mistake (it adds Eternal Venom stacks).
